    The guard notes that a recommendation to put an LPA in place is future planning, not evidence of current third-party support, and that the LPA here is held by the client (preparedness), not over the client. The report's LPA disclosure therefore does not constitute a current support arrangement and does not address the FG21/1 vulnerability overlay. The main findings are: (1) There is no vulnerability assessment anywhere in the case file. The extraction confirms /vulnerability/assessment as NotPresentInDocument, and no equivalent document (FG21/1 checklist, vulnerability screening, adviser notes on driver indicators) appears in any retrieved passage. This is not an extraction failure — the extraction explicitly flags it as absent from the document. (2) The client's self-reported knowledge profile (no understanding of stocks and shares, very little understanding of pension planning, P1/P3) is a clear low-capability driver under FG21/1. Neither the report nor the file records what steps were taken to support the client's understanding, whether comprehension was checked, or whether any adjustments were considered. The fact find records 'No Requirement' for accessible format and 'No Preference' for delivery method, but this captures preference, not an assessed conclusion following a vulnerability conversation. (3) The LPA claim is asserted by the report but not corroborated by any file document. No fact-find entry, sight note or other evidence of a registered LPA is visible in the retrieved passages. The assertion is therefore unsupported. The communication preferences recorded in the fact find (no requirement, no preference) are consistent with the report's silence on adjustments, so no contradiction arises there, but the absence of any process record showing FG21/1 considerations were worked through remains a concern. Taken together, the absence of any vulnerability assessment in a case carrying a low-capability FG21/1 driver, combined with an unsupported LPA assertion, constitutes a moderate-to-high concern.

These are squarely within FG21/1's low capability indicators. Under PRIN 2A.2.1R and FCA FG21/1, where a client presents with low financial capability, the firm should consider what adjustments support informed decision-making and record them. The fact find (P2) records 'Accessible format requirement: No Requirement' and 'Preferred delivery method: No Preference', which establishes that communication preferences were asked about and the client stated none. That satisfies the communication preferences limb at a basic level. The ATRQ self-completion without adviser oversight (P2, P16) is a further process note: no record confirms the client understood the questionnaire or that the adviser verified the answers, which matters given the low knowledge baseline. The guard on standard cancellation rights does not apply here. The 'No Requirement / No Preference' entries in the fact find (P2) address format preferences but do not constitute a vulnerability assessment or a record of comprehension adjustments. The low capability indicator is present in the file and is unaddressed in the report's reasoning or support measures. This is a Moderate finding: support needs linked to low financial capability are recorded in the file but not reflected in the report's communication adjustments or reasoning.

  [G10.4] Vulnerability has a material impact on the recommendation (overlay)
  Potential Concern — severity High
    Report:   The report asserts two foreseeable harms (both inferred, not explicitly stated as vulnerability findings): (1) the client has limited investment knowledge and understanding, creating risk of not fully understanding the recommended SIPP, GIA and ISA products; mitigation stated as documented knowledge/experience assessment, product literature provided, and suitability report explaining features, risks and charges. (2) Proposed charges including 1% ongoing adviser fee are higher than current arrangement; mitigation stated as critical yield analysis in appendix and adviser justification by reference to improved risk alignment and professional oversight.     File:     The fact find [P14] records: 'Accessible format requirement: No Requirement' and 'Preferred delivery method: No Preference'.     Discrepancies:
      - The report records foreseeable harms referencing limited investment knowledge but frames these as inferred risk disclosures rather than the output of a structured vulnerability assessment; no vulnerability assessment document or section appears in any retrieved passage.
      - The extraction confirms /vulnerability/assessment is NotPresentInDocument, indicating the report itself contains no formal vulnerability assessment section, not merely an extraction failure.
      - The capacity for loss questionnaire [P1] records no additional notes, yet the report asserts a Medium-High capacity for loss with a specific rationale about guaranteed income — this rationale appears only in the suitability report itself with no corroborating discussion record.
      - The agreed risk level and investment target discussion sections [P12] both record 'No additional notes were recorded', yet the report describes a nuanced discussion about risk and capacity; this is an internal consistency point rather than a direct contradiction but leaves the discussion unsubstantiated by the file.
      - No FG21/1 vulnerability screening output, vulnerability identification, or vulnerability impact on recommendation assessment is present anywhere in the case file, despite the client exhibiting at least one FG21/1 low-capability driver (self-declared very low investment knowledge/understanding).

      - No record of third-party authority, Power of Attorney, trusted contacts or representative involvement appears anywhere in the file, and the report makes no assertion about these — this absence is consistent but unassessed.
    The client carries a clear FG21/1 low-capability driver: the risk profile questionnaire [P2] records 'No understanding / knowledge' of stocks and shares and EIS/VCTs, and 'Very little understanding / knowledge' of pension planning. These are self-declared capability deficits that, under FG21/1, require the adviser to consider whether the client is a vulnerable customer whose needs should shape the advice process and the recommendation itself. The suitability report acknowledges the low knowledge in its Knowledge & Experience section and frames it as a foreseeable harm with a stated mitigation. However, neither the report nor any document in the file shows a structured vulnerability assessment was conducted or that the FG21/1 overlay was formally applied. The /vulnerability/assessment path is confirmed absent from the document (NotPresentInDocument), meaning the report is silent on this point, not merely unread by the extractor. The capacity for loss questionnaire [P1] records no additional notes, and the agreed risk level section [P12] records no additional notes — these are the natural places where low-capability indicators would be discussed and documented. The report's Medium-High capacity for loss conclusion and its description of a nuanced risk discussion therefore rest entirely on the suitability report's own narrative with no corroboration. Under the rules of this assessment, the report cannot corroborate itself. The age guard is not engaged here — this is not an age-only concern; it is a documented capability deficit. The 'vulnerability genuinely absent and assessed' guard is also not available: the case file shows no assessment was conducted. The communication preferences recorded in [P14] (no requirement, no preference) are consistent with the report not making adjustments, but the absence of any reflection in the report on how the client's low knowledge was accommodated — beyond providing product literature — is a disclosure gap. The recommendation places the client into three new wrappers (SIPP, GIA, ISA) with higher charges than the existing arrangement. For a client with very low investment knowledge, this requires the adviser to demonstrate that the client understood what they were agreeing to. The foreseeable harm disclosures in the canonical model are inferred assertions; they are not the output of a conducted assessment. The overall picture is: clear FG21/1 low-capability driver present and documented in the file, no formal vulnerability assessment conducted or recorded, no impact-on-recommendation analysis, and no documented process adjustment beyond standard product literature. This is a High severity finding under the guidance.

  [G10.5] Vulnerabilities recorded in the file and in the report
  Potential Concern — severity High
    Report:   The canonical model records hasVulnerability as 'No' for one client and 'NotAssessed' for the other (presumably Anthony Barnes and Yvette Barnes respectively). The paths for vulnerability categories, characteristics and assessment date are all absent from the extracted model, and the extraction's own report confirms /vulnerability/assessment was 'NotPresentInDocument'. The suitability report passage [P18] discusses risk profile, capacity for loss, knowledge and experience, but contains no explicit vulnerability assessment section, no reference to FG21/1 characteristics, and no record of communication adjustments or support needs.
    File:     The fact-find [P1] records 'Accessible format requirement: No Requirement' and 'Preferred delivery method: No Preference', and shows that the ATRQ was not completed at the meeting — it was emailed to the client to complete himself. [P7] records the meeting as a telephone call with nobody else present and no third-party authority noted. [P3] records that the client 'Client will complete ATRQ in his own time'. [P8] and [P9] (adviser notes) describe the client as retired but working part-time, about to receive a large inheritance, and asking about investment of £60k — a life event (retirement transition, inheritance receipt) that is a recognised FG21/1 driver. The cashflow files [P14] reference a future 'Illness' event at age 104 (a modelling default, not a clinical indicator). No vulnerability screening form, no formal FG21/1 assessment, and no record of a vulnerability check or outcome is present anywhere in the case file. The fact-find contains no dedicated vulnerability section with a recorded outcome.
    Discrepancies:
      - The canonical model records hasVulnerability as 'NotAssessed' for one client, indicating no formal assessment was conducted, yet no vulnerability assessment document or screening record exists anywhere in the case file to confirm even a negative outcome was properly reached.
      - The client is in a retirement transition (recently retired, part-time work ending, planning full retirement by 60) and is receiving a large inheritance (£100,000) — both are recognised FG21/1 life-event drivers — yet neither the report nor any case-file document contains a vulnerability assessment or acknowledgement of these drivers.
      - The ATRQ was not completed at the point of meeting (sent by email for the client to complete later per [P1] and [P3]), which is itself a process deviation that could indicate a support or comprehension issue, yet no follow-up or note of outcome is recorded.
      - The suitability report [P18] discusses knowledge and experience (very limited investment understanding) which is a FG21/1 capability driver, but there is no documented link between this finding and a vulnerability or capability assessment under FG21/1.
      - No Power of Attorney, trusted contact, or third-party authority is recorded in any document, which is consistent with the report, but the file contains no affirmative record that this was asked and confirmed absent.
    The report records 'No' vulnerability for one party and 'NotAssessed' for the other. The extraction confirms /vulnerability/assessment is not present in the document. The case file similarly contains no vulnerability screening form, no FG21/1 assessment outcome, and no record of a formal vulnerability check. On the 'both absent' rule this would ordinarily be no issue — but the decision logic requires me to look at whether clear FG21/1 drivers are present in the file that the report never reflects. They are: (1) life event — the client is transitioning into retirement and receiving a large inheritance this month [P8], both of which are named FG21/1 drivers; (2) low capability — the report itself [P18] records very limited investment knowledge and experience, which is a FG21/1 low-capability indicator; (3) the ATRQ was not completed in the meeting and was sent for self-completion [P1][P3], a process deviation that could itself signal a comprehension or engagement issue. These are not historic or resolved — they are contemporaneous with the advice. The absence of any vulnerability assessment in a case carrying at least two clear FG21/1 drivers (life event, low capability) means the 'NotAssessed' outcome is not a considered negative finding but a gap. The 'accessible format: No Requirement' and 'No Preference' delivery entries [P1] are the only communication-preference records in the file and were recorded, which is positive, but they do not substitute for a vulnerability screen. The guard noting that a resolved or historic vulnerability may no longer apply does not assist here because no vulnerability was ever recorded. The guard about age alone not being vulnerability is not engaged. The net position is: clear FG21/1 drivers are present in the file and are not reflected in any vulnerability assessment, and the suitability report neither acknowledges them nor records a considered finding that they do not meet the vulnerability threshold. This is a High concern under the severity guidance (no vulnerability assessment in a case with clear FG21/1 drivers).
